### Engine batch 18 merged, queue D stopped, discards moved, city lights (03:06 to 03:20 UTC)

- 03:06 Phase B committed (cee8906), main a1cffed merged (696e231), pushed. Queue D had rendered 1755 (sha256 afbe29da; against the 2048 Moon tree only the Moon and its bloom differ: 3530 pixels, 119 by more than 2 levels, 67 pixels by one level further than 60 px from the Moon) and 2010.
- Mail 63 (03:10 UTC, no reply needed): engine batch 18 is on main (a67c9c34): hold, dive and terrain frames keep their bytes, the judged rise frame gets a sky lift of 0.74. Finding from Saturn: on the harness GPU a pixel that hit `discard` stops storing, so `dFdx`, `dFdy`, `fwidth` and implicit level `texture()` of its live quad neighbours read stale memory. Rule: no `discard` before the last statement of a fragment shader, no derivative or implicit level `texture()` on a value first assigned inside a per pixel branch. Fix with the next commit and say in the next READY which frames changed and by how many pixels.
- Queue D stopped by hand (its pictures would be from a tree without batch 18). Merged main 0622510 (holds batch 18) as 40f2b71, pushed; build passes, vitest 79 of 79.
- Discards in my folder, all five moved behind the last statement: `air.ts` and `veil.ts` (`uDiag >= 2`, a uniform, was the first statement; the early `return` of the air shader became an `else`), `moon.ts` (edge cover, was before `outColor`), `relief.ts` ground (`uDiag >= 2 && ground < 0.5`, was before `diagTexels(uv * uSize)`, so the G13 view could read stale derivatives at the horizon) and towers (`texel.a < 0.004`, was before `clear`, `lens` and `diagTexels`; the beauty value does not use a derivative after it, the G13 view does). Derivatives and implicit level `texture()` elsewhere: `aurora()` takes `dFdx(uv)` at its top level, the patch levels and the far maps use explicit gradients inside uniform branches or with gradients taken outside the per pixel loop, `air-glow.png`, `relief-masks.png` and `relief-low.webp` have no mipmaps. Nothing else to change.
- City lights (item 8; round 27 R3: "uniform round dots of similar size and brightness" at 1755, "oversized soft yellow blobs with blocky pixel halos and isolated square bright dots" at 2156). New tool `assets-src/earth/lightsize.py STILL` (hero against film on the hold's night side, lights as excess over a 15 px median): hero 625 px over 0.3, 232 over 0.5, 18 over 0.8, the 15 brightest (250, 240, 210) with 11.5 px over half of their peak; film before 882, 511, 208, the 15 brightest (255, 255, 254) with 7.5 px. So the film's bright lights are clipped white squares where the hero's are warm and wider.
- Cause: `city.npy` is the light of the 2 times enlarged hero, whose light cores are white and clip at the map's peak (5 in every channel); the film averages them in scene light, the hero at film size is an average of display values. Cure at the asset, not a filter in the shader: `wrap.py emissive_map` low passes the city light in display coding (`CITY_SOFT`, a Gaussian in pixels of the enlarged hero) before it is decoded and mapped; the lights gain (`look.globe.twinkle[3]`, was 0.7) is set from the energy against the hero. `wrap.py 8192 emissive` repeats the committed map byte for byte without the step (checked) and takes 11 s.
- Seen on the way (`out/work/earth/pb/l1/city-1755.png`): at film px (1375, 545) the hero has a green fan with white wisps, the film a pale smudge with the wisps (the green is taken out by `look.globe.calm`, round 19 item 6). Known trade, stays.

### Mail 80, the arrival 1666 to 1679: looked at on this branch, cause, plan (06:10 to 06:30 UTC)

- Queue: the full format rise cover sheet is put behind this item. q7 rendered 1666 to 1679 on this branch (code 3b3819e, engine batch 19) at the review tier, `--format preview`, to `out/work/earth/pb/h/a-F.png` (1668 is `pb/f/p-1668.png`), about 55 s each. q8 renders the same fourteen frames in the work tree `/home/user/workspace/earth-wt` set to main 89715a1 (the kept Earth the critic saw) to `pb/h/b-F.png`.
- Main moved to 89715a1 (PROGRESS, status pictures, `assets-src/shared/ROUND31-FIXES.md`; no engine or stop code): merged as d63e142, pushed. PROGRESS says issue 4 of round 30 (the hero seen again at 1857 and 2156) is held by the Builder against the kit's shape of a stop; nothing for me there.
- Samples at review, motion in the shutter: 1666 17.0 px, 1667 15.7, 1668 15.1, 1669 13.1, 1670 10.8, 1671 8.7, 1672 7.0, 1673 5.0, 1674 3.4, 1675 2.1, 1676 1.1, 1677 0.5, 1678 0.2, 1679 0.1. Earth is at 0.78 of its hold size at 1666, so "small" is the sheet tile, not the body.
- Seen on this branch: at 1666 to 1669 the shutter draws the rays of the aurora into one green band along the limb, with the red glow outside it; a second green band lies inside the limb on the disc (the glow of the emissive map's alpha, `uAuroraColor` in globe.ts). From 1672 the rays stand as rays. At sheet tile size (480 px wide and smaller) every one of the fourteen frames shows a green patch on the upper right limb, with or without rays.
- Cause: the aurora is at its full hold strength through the whole arrival, while the body moves up to 17 px in the shutter; what makes it read as an aurora (rays, ripple) cannot be seen in a moving, small body, only its colours along the limb.
- Cure planned (no shader change, so the judged frames keep their bytes by construction): the aurora rises after the camera has come to rest. `look.onset = [-0.1334, 1.2, 0.6]`: seconds about the start of the hold between which the strength goes from 0 to 1 (frames 1676 to 1716, smooth), and the ray height at the start as a share of the full height. In set.ts `born = smooth(...)` multiplies the gain of the air shell's aurora and `uAuroraColor` of the globe (the glow on the disc); aurora.ts scales `uAuroraSpan.z, .w` (low, top) by `1 - (1 - 0.6) * (1 - born)`, so the rays grow to their height while they brighten. From frame 1716 on every uniform has its old value exactly (times 1, minus 0).
- Scratch copies of the three files with the change: `out/work/earth/pb/h/new/`. Applied after the before strip ends (no `src` edit while a render runs).

### Mail 80: before strip seen, cure built (06:30 to 06:55 UTC)

- Before strip (main 89715a1 in the work tree, kept Earth, `pb/h/b-F.png`) beside this branch as it was (`pb/h/a-F.png`): the same thing on both. Main has the curtain mesh with red blotches at the ray tops, the branch has the map rays with the soft red glow; on both a green band lies along the upper right limb in all fourteen frames, smeared at 1666 to 1669. So the critic's line holds for the branch too. `pix.py` b-1668 against p-1668: 38,966 pixels differ, so the work tree renders its own tree (the isolation check of the discards stays valid).
- First try (gain of the shell aurora and `uAuroraColor` times `born`, ray height from 0.6): the rays and the red are gone at 1679, but a broad green band stays on the night disc. It is not the emissive alpha term: it is baked into `wrap-surface.jpg` (the hero's own pixels; green excess up to 70 code values in 20 px cells at lon -40 to -5, lat 28 to 65) and a little into `wrap-cloud.webp`. Draft `pb/i/d-1679.png`, map picture `pb/i/v-wrap-green.png`.
- Second try: limit green on the disc with the round 3 mask (`keep` times `ramp(uAuroraGate, ndl)`): halves the green excess but leaves the band's day end (ndl over 0.05) and its low end (lat under 38, outside `keep`). Dropped.
- Kept: a cap of its own. `look.globe.bed = [-20, 46, 30, 0.25, 0.5]`: lon, lat, radius in degrees of a cap over the baked band (full inside 0.6 of the radius, as the calm spots), and the light range (ndl) over which it ends toward the day, so the day side's green land (Newfoundland, the east coast) keeps its colour. globe.ts: uniforms `uUnborn`, `uBedAt`, `uBedNight`; one line after the calm spots, inside a loop that runs only while `uUnborn > 0`: `col.g = mix(col.g, min(col.g, max(col.r, col.b) * uCalm), uUnborn * cap * night)`. No texture call, no derivative, no per pixel branch. With `uUnborn = 0` (every frame from 1716 on) the line is not run.
- Draft 1679 with the cure (`pb/i/g-1679.png`, pictures `v-bed-1.png`, `v-bed-2.png`, `v-rim-1.png`): no green band, no red, no rays; where the band was the night disc is a soft grey teal haze (green equal to blue), the day side's land unchanged (difference picture is confined to the band). Green excess in the crop 600 by 520: 241,560 before, vegetation only after.
- Onset on drafts (first variant, `pb/i/v-onset-2.png`): 1686 faint short rays, 1692 to 1704 rays grow and brighten, 1710 nearly full, 1716 full.
- Files changed: `src/stops/earth/{set.ts, aurora.ts, globe.ts, look.ts}`. No asset changed.

- The flag of mail 138, read in the code on main (`src/space/sky.ts`, `unfolded(s, p, t, gx, gy, m, shift)` with `m = uPatchMode.z`, which is the patch's `edge`, and `shift = unfold[0]`; `unfold[1]` moves the start of the outer fade in `patchCoord`): the far texel is taken only where the patch coordinate is within `edge` of the patch's outer side, and faded in over the first 0.35 of that zone. Mars passes `edge: def.margin / wide`, so its zone is its whole margin. Earth's terrain patch has margin 0.5 (0.25 of the patch, 1408 px) and `edge` 0.05 (282 px of 5632 at the outer side, 158 px of 3168 at the top): the zone lies 1126 px or more outside the approved border, and the margin in view reaches at most 157 patch px above the border and 53 px left of it. So the flag as built cannot touch the twin (34 px outside the border) or the cut at the top border. Raising `edge` to the whole margin would not cure them either: the twin would keep 98.6 percent of its weight (34 of 493 px of the crossfade), the patch would fade against the engine's sky across the margin (weight 0.90 at the top of frame 2100; the wide margin with the narrow edge was made at 08:05 of the first build because the rise showed the engine's sky beside the Sun), and the painted glow above the top border would be replaced by image texels from further down (60 percent at the top of frame 2100). To be proved with pictures as mail 138 asks (flag on, 2099 and 2125), then the asset side cure with the same pictures.

No mirror in it: the flag is not asked for the orbit patch.

## Risk notes (d), worked while q25 renders (13:15 UTC on, no renders, no `src` change)

- (d2) The rise's start. `pb/k2/leave.ts` (tsx, the plan class with two `leave` laws; skyline of the sea in the frame, px at 1080 rows, per frame): the present law (`from: 6.5, ramp: 3`) moves the skyline by 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 1.4 px per frame at 2121 to 2124, then 13.3, 34.7, 46.6, 47.4, 47.5 (2129): three frames from rest to full speed, 21 px per frame per frame at the most. With `from: 10, ramp: 10`: 0.8 (2121), 3.4, 7.9, 13.8, 20.5, 27.5, 34.2, 40.1, 44.7 (2129): nine frames, at most 7 px per frame per frame. The family that keeps the centre: the lowering at the centre is `rate (from - ramp / 2)` and its slope is `rate` when `from >= ramp`, so `from = 5 + ramp / 2` and `ramp <= 10`; (10, 10) is the longest ramp that keeps both.
- Proved by the same script: `handover.height` is the same double (1.035878881865979), the pose (position, quaternion, field of view) is equal to the bit at 2100 to 2120 and from 2130 on, so only the nine frames 2121 to 2129 change, by 0.3 mrad (2121) to 21.5 mrad (2125) of pitch. None is a judged frame. G13 counts the rise's frames outside the cover window, so the measure runs again on the tree with the change.
- What to look at in the strip 2119 to 2131 (review, preview format) before keeping it: the top margin of the terrain sky comes into view 1 to 1.5 frames earlier under a thinner deck (at 2125 the skyline is 30 px lower than now), the bottom apron, no step at 2120 to 2121 or 2129 to 2130.
- (d1) The dive deck's rays. Engine `src/terrain/cover.ts`: `rush(p, k)` reads a noise at (direction from the focus times k.x, log(radius) k.y - phase k.z); the phase is `speed dt`. With my `coverRush` (speed 2.4, streak [9, 2.2, 1.6], billow [2.2, 0.9, 1]) the fine pattern moves 0.128 of a noise cell per frame along the ray and the billow 0.08: one cell in 8 and in 12 frames, on a cover of 12 frames. That is why the rays read as standing. A higher speed is the lever in my folder (it moves every frame of both covers, none judged). Kept for round 35 unless round 33's motion critic does not name it: it needs both cover sheets at review as proof (about 35 min), which does not fit beside step 1 and (d2) before 15:05.

## (d1) the covers' rays, measured (14:10 to 14:20 UTC)

- Drafts, full format, 24 frames each at `coverRush.speed` 2.4, 5, 8 (`pb/k4/v2.4`, `v5`, `v8`; `pb/k4/rays.py` gives the correlation of the high pass picture, 40 px, between a frame and the one before, on the deck only). Dive 1884 to 1887: 0.74, 0.75, 0.74, 0.71 at 2.4; 0.72, 0.72, 0.72, 0.71 at 5; 0.70, 0.69, 0.70, 0.68 at 8. So the speeds I planned hardly move it. Rise 2129 to 2132: 0.20, 0.21, 0.21, 0.10 at 2.4; 0.13, 0.11, 0.11, 0.03 at 8 (the rise's rays do not stand).
- Why (`pb/k4/flow.ts`, the plan's own poses): the rush pattern runs outward at `speed k.z / k.y` per second in log radius: 1.75 (fine) and 2.67 (billow) at speed 2.4, that is 6 and 9 percent of the radius per frame. The world under the camera grows at speed over height: dive orbit side 4 per second at 1877, 8 (1879), 15 (1880), 27 (1881), 31 (1882), 10 (1883), so 27 to 100 percent of the radius per frame; rise orbit side 12 (2131), 19, 19, 13, 10, 8, 6 (2137). On the terrain sides the camera is slow (rise 1.7 to 2 per second at 2120 to 2129, dive nearly at rest from 1885). So 2.4 fits the terrain sides and is 5 to 10 times too slow for the plunge: near the centre the fan of rays is the same picture for five frames. The phase is 0 at the centre for any speed, so only the rate per frame counts: `speed k.z / 30` of a noise cell.
- The speed also moves where the cover opens (its alpha reads the same noise): at 2132 and 2133 the deck with speed 8 hides the globe where 2.4 shows it through holes. So every cover frame has to be looked at for any new speed.
- 14:15 `pb/q28b.sh` (same frames, speeds 12 and 20, `pb/k4/v12`, `v20`; then back to 2.4 and a build; `done` in `pb/k4/q.log`). Decision rule: keep a higher speed only if the strips show the fan changing from frame to frame with no flicker of the whole deck and no worse opening; else 2.4 stays and (d1) is reported as measured and left.
- 14:20 q28b `done` (speeds 12 and 20, `pb/k4/v12`, `v20`). **The correlation number was the wrong tool**: at 1884 to 1887 it stays at 0.63 to 0.70 even at speed 20, because on the terrain side of the dive the camera is nearly at rest and the high pass picture is the terrain seen through the thinning cover, not the rays. Judged by the strips instead (`pb/k4/strips.py 2.4 8 12 20` -> `pb/k4/strip-{dive,rise}-{frames,high}.png`):
  - Dive, high pass: at 2.4 the fan at the upper right is the same picture at 1882, 1883, 1884 (and 1885, the centre is frame 1885.0, where every speed gives the same pattern); at 12 other rays in each frame, still plain; at 20 the fan is nearly smoothed away by the shutter. In the plain frames the dive's deck looks alike at every speed (the rays are 3 levels rms).
  - Rise terrain side 2127 to 2130: at 2.4 strong dark rays from below that stand at 2129 to 2131; at 8 and 12 softer, other rays each frame.
  - Rise orbit side 2131 to 2136: at 2.4 the deck opens in holes that sit still on the screen while the globe falls away at 12 to 19 per second (globe through a hole at 2131 already, dense band left at 2136); at 8 and 12 the deck is whole to 2133 and thins evenly as streaming mist over 2134 to 2136 and on.
- Which speed fits: billow runs at 1.11 times speed, fine at 0.727 times speed per second; the world runs at 15 to 31 (dive, 1880 to 1882) and 12 to 19 (rise, 2131 to 2134). Speed 12 gives 13.3 and 8.7. **Candidate: 12.**
- Where the speed reaches (from `set.ts`, `veil.ts`): my veil is drawn from 15 frames before each window to 0.85 s (dive) and 0.5 s (rise) after it. Dive: the orbit side reads the rush only once `layer.rush` is above 0 (from about 1878) and the terrain side of the dive uses the near wisps (no rush), so the dive changes at 1878 to 1890 only. Rise: the rush is the only pattern on both sides, so 2108 to 2153 change: the thin wisps over the end of the terrain view (2108 to 2122) and the tail over the globe (2138 to 2153) too. 2156 and 1857 must not change.
- 14:25 `pb/q30.sh` (folder `pb/k5`, log `pb/k5/q.log`): drafts of 44 frames (1874 to 1878, 1890 to 1900, 2108 to 2124 every frame, 2137 to 2154 every frame, 2156) at speed 12, then at 2.4 (`pb/k5/v12`, `v2.4`); ends with `plan.ts` back at 2.4 and built, `done`. To look at: flicker of thin wisps at 2108 to 2124, the tail 2137 to 2153, and which frames are byte equal.
- Looks pushed 14:25 (the two trial strips).
- Time plan from here (CUT34 about 16:15): if 12 is kept: both cover sheets at review, preview format (38 min), then ONE final proof (measure 28 min, finals 13 min, main drafts 2 min) starting 15:25 at the latest. Critics' words that need more than a trivial change go to round 35 with a note in CUT34; a head is only sent proved.
- 14:33 q30 `done`. Byte equal between speed 2.4 and 12 at draft: 1874, 1891, 1892, 1900, 2108 to 2113, 2147 to 2154, 2156 (571d6cd6, as before). Moved: 1876 to 1890 and 2114 to 2146. So the reach is narrower than the veil's own window.
- **Speed 12 has a cost**: the billow (2.2 cells around, 0.9 per e fold: one or two cells over the whole frame) sets how thick the whole veil is, and at speed 12 it changes 5 times faster. Mean level of the middle half of the frame, 2137 to 2146: at 2.4: 184.9, 172.7, 158.2, 142.4, 125.5, 110.6, 99.8, 93.9, 91.5, 91.0 (steps 12 to 17, even); at 12: 184.2, 170.2, 157.8, 137.0, 108.7, 94.3, 92.9, 93.0, 91.5, 91.0 (steps 21 and 28 at 2140 and 2141, then flat): the veil clears two frames sooner and in a jerk. The lead 2114 to 2124 grows less evenly too (steps 2.5, 3.3, 5.1, 4.5, 4.9, 2.3, 4.9, 4.7, 0.2, 0.7 against 3.1, 4.4, 5.3, 5.0, 4.8, 4.3, 3.6, 2.6, 1.7, 1.4). **Speed 12 thrown away.**
- **Third candidate: only the fine pattern faster**: `streak: [9, 2.2, 8]` (was 1.6), speed stays 2.4. The standing rays are the fine pattern (about 23 rays per radian: its first two octaves); the billow, which opens and thins the cover, keeps its pace, so the opening and the thinning stay as they are. Fine pattern: 0.64 cell per frame (was 0.128), 8.7 per second outward (was 1.75), as with speed 12.

- major, sheet-05.png (earth.rise 2100 to 2174), background starfield and Milky Way, frames 2147-2174 vs sheet-01.png/sheet-02.png: "the Milky Way jumps ~45 degrees from a top-left-to-bottom-right diagonal before the dive to a nearly vertical band behind Earth after the rise despite returning to the same globe framing" Fix named: "lock the starfield skybox in world coordinates across all segments so the background stars and Milky Way do not reorient across the stop"
- major, sheet-04.png (earth.terrain 1920 to 2099), cloud field, shadows, and ocean surface across frames 1920-2099: "clouds, cloud shadows, and ocean surface detail are frozen onto static geometry with no cloud drift or water flow, while Earth's globe rotation in sheet-05.png (2147-2174) also stalls relative to its rate in sheet-01.png" Fix named: "animate subtle cloud drift and ocean specular flow in the terrain view and advance planetary rotation at a consistent rate across the entire stop"
- minor, sheet-01.png (earth.hold 1680 to 1829), upper-right limb aurora, frames 1680-1721: "the green aurora is nearly absent on frame 1680 and ramps in abruptly over frames 1680-1721 before holding steady" Fix named: "start the aurora at steady-state intensity at the beginning of hold or evolve its curtains smoothly without an initial fade-in pop"
Builder's note: the third severe is about what the cover sheet shows (the harness draws the twelve frames of the cover window, 1879 to 1890), not about the picture; it stays out of your list and is written down as it is. The two severe on the dive and the rise are items 54 and 55 in new words: say in your READY whether your second take b6764ba4 answers them as it stands (what 1862 to 1895 and 2113 to 2147 look like in it), and what of the four majors (stepped ghost copies 2132 to 2136, the Moon pinned while Earth grows 1830 to 1870 and at 2140, the band of the sky turned about 45 degrees between hold and rise, clouds and ocean frozen over 1920 to 2099) it carries or can carry by CUT34. Motion comes first, as Brandon ordered."

- So: motion first. CUT34 not before 15:50, 20 minutes of notice. The CUT34 message must say: whether b6764ba4 answers the two severe as it stands (what 1862 to 1895 and 2113 to 2147 look like in it), and for each of the four majors what the head carries or can carry.

## MAIL 174 from the Builder (14:54 UTC, high priority, newest mail, binding; verbatim)

"MAIL 174 to earth_builder, 14:54 UTC. CUT34 is at 16:20 UTC (round 34, polish 2 of 3). Your `CUT34 <folder>` message is due by 16:15 UTC at the latest; what is not READY then waits for CUT35, the last cut.

What the message holds, as for CUT33: the commit to take (the exact hash on your branch, merged with origin/main as it stands when you send), each separable change as a commit of its own so that it can be taken or left alone, the judged frames that differ from main at the final tier with the reason, the judged frames that keep main's bytes, the segments whose motion changed, the open items and the round 33 words it answers, and what got worse or is new, in your own words. If your change sits on another agent's commit (sky, travel, belt, the Neptune arrival swing), name that commit.

Order of worth, unchanged: motion first (dives, covers, handovers, the terrain flights), then severe, then major. Nothing that costs R1 at a hold or a terrain frame of a finished stop (floors: sun 9, mercury 9, venus 8, earth 9, jupiter 10, saturn 6, uranus 9, neptune 7; Mars is not touched). Round 35 is the last round of the polish and has no round behind it to mend a loss. It takes only small proven things and take-outs. So what matters should be in CUT34. No answer needed to this mail."

- severe, sheet-06.png (earth.rise 2125 to 2136), frames 2125-2136 across the rise cover (and sheet-05.png frames 2100-2147): "the camera reverses direction across the cover: frames 2100-2131 accelerate forward and downward into the terrain until the horizon exits the top edge at 2131, then frames 2135-2147 cut to a backward pull-out from the full globe in space, while zoomed-in reef streaks ghost in the lower corners of 2133-2136 over the emerging orbital globe" Fix named: "animate rise as one continuous upward and outward pull-back from the terrain through volumetric clouds into orbit so camera direction, horizon position, and velocity carry smoothly across the cover without double-exposing the two shots"
- severe, sheet-03.png (earth.dive 1879 to 1890), frames 1879-1890 across the dive cover (and sheet-02.png frames 1870-1895): "between 1870 and 1879 the globe pops into an opaque, featureless white sphere with a bright cyan upper-sky glow and steep limb curvature, then across 1879-1883 the cyan sky abruptly drops to black while the horizon flattens and shifts down into a low-orbit plate whose top-center sun contradicts the left-lit orbital globe" Fix named: "align sun direction, sky exposure, and horizon curvature between the orbital globe and low-orbit view, and pass continuously through layered clouds instead of popping to a solid white sphere and warping the limb across four frames"
- major, sheet-05.png (earth.rise 2100 to 2174), frames 2147-2174 background starfield and globe interior compared with sheet-01.png and sheet-02.png: "after returning to the same orbital framing and Moon position, the Milky Way band behind Earth has jumped roughly 40 degrees clockwise from diagonal to nearly vertical, while Earth's longitudinal rotation has snapped back to its pre-dive 1829 angle instead of advancing continuously across the 345-frame visit" Fix named: "lock the starfield and Milky Way orientation in world space across the entire stop and drive Earth's rotation from a single continuous clock through hold, dive, terrain, and rise"
- major, sheet-04.png (earth.terrain 1920 to 2099), frames 1920-2099 cumulus clouds over islands in lower half and sun flare at top-center edge: "cumulus clouds are baked flat onto the ocean and island surface with zero drift and zero altitude parallax relative to their shadows beneath them, while an unanchored white sun flare slides into frame from the top-center border over 2034-2099" Fix named: "place clouds on an elevated layer above the sea mesh so they show subtle vertical parallax and gentle drift relative to their surface shadows, and lock the sun flare consistently to the sky and horizon geometry"
- minor, sheet-02.png (earth.dive 1830 to 1919), frame 1870 lower-right city lights and right-limb aurora (and sheet-06.png frames 2134-2136 lower corners): "high-speed radial zoom blur breaks bright city lights, reef highlights, and aurora features into coarse stepped streaks" Fix named: "increase temporal motion-blur sampling during peak dive and rise speeds so bright point features and edges smear into a smooth continuous blur"
- minor, sheet-01.png (earth.hold 1680 to 1829), frames 1680-1721 upper-right limb: "the green aurora starts near zero at frame 1680 and ramps up sharply over the first 40 frames of hold rather than reading as ongoing planetary motion" Fix named: "begin hold with the aurora already at its steady baseline brightness and animate only gentle curtain drift"
R3-part2, score 6:
- major, image-09.png (venus.travel 1642), Earth globe, lower half and centre (crops A, Q): "city lights read as a bright gold metallic foil: they sit on the lit side of the disc instead of only on the night side, and they come out as thick glowing worm shapes, not fine networks of points. The whole globe looks airbrushed and plastic, with cloud swirls smeared over land with no real depth" Fix named: "mask the night-lights texture to the night side of the terminator with a soft falloff, lower its intensity and use a finer point-based lights map; give the clouds sharper detail, cast shadows and a proper specular/normal response"
- minor, image-09.png (venus.travel 1642), small moon below and right of Earth (about 822,486; crops A, H): "the moon is an 8 to 10 px grey blob with a mushy outline and no surface detail or clear phase edge, and it looks like a smudge" Fix named: "render the moon larger or with a sharp crescent terminator and a clean edge that is consistent with the sun direction"
R5, score 6:

The journey goes in and then back out." Fix named: "Leave Earth outward after the ocean pass. Do not reprint the orbital portrait."

The sky jumps rather than staying put" Fix named: "lock the galaxy backdrop to the world-space sky sphere so it only moves with camera rotation; do not fade or swap it per shot"
The jury on your hold and terrain frame, 2 of 10 for film (a a b b b b b d d d). Words on the hold 1755: "The aurora floats in a detached green band off the limb. (d); "The impossible postcard stack: full daylight continents, night-side city lights, aurora, a cratered moon, and a bright Milky Way all perfectly exposed together" (b); "exaggerated mountain relief, daylit continents, night-side city lights, limb aurora, a bright Milky Way backdrop, and an oversized Moon tucked into the corner" (b). ### 18:00 to 18:10 UTC: mail 189 (a) read by sun angle; queue q6; ending frames

- **Where the warmth of 1755 sits** (final 143b0a53 against the hero at film size, both blurred 12 px, display levels, disc with view cosine over 0.35; script in the session, numbers here): by sun cosine (ndl) the film minus the hero is: -0.2 to 0: +1.2 R, 0.0 G, -1.4 B; **0 to 0.1: +8.3 R, +3.4 G, -2.0 B**; 0.1 to 0.2: +4.1, +2.5, +1.2; 0.2 to 0.4: +2.0, +1.6, +1.3; 0.4 to 0.6: +1.8, +0.7, +0.7; 0.6 to 0.8: +2.0, 0.0, 0.0; 0.8 to 1: +2.4, +1.6, +2.1. On the limb ring (view cosine under 0.35, about 55,000 px) +5 to +10 in all three channels at ndl 0 to 0.4 and 0.8 to 1 (the air's rim, not the curve). So the red is a broad +2 over the day side and a strong warm band at the terminator, whose proportions are the twilight term's (`twilight: [0.3, 0.06, -0.1, ...]`).
- **Why `trim.py` on the final asks 13 to 20 percent**: it compares sums of linear light, and the film is crisper than the hero, so its peaks weigh more in linear light (sum ratio hero / film 0.82 to 0.86, but the median of the per pixel ratio is 0.96 to 1.02 and the ratio of blurred display means 0.96 to 0.99). So the sum fit is fooled by the micro contrast; it is not the tool for this.
- **Candidate, not applied, not rendered**: `out/work/earth/pb/c35/trimfit.py STILL OUT` (run with `PYTHONPATH=assets-src/earth` from that folder) fits multipliers of the light curve per knot from ndl 0 up so that the blurred display means per knot meet the hero's (8 passes, exponent 1.6, smoothed as trim.py does), predicts the frame by decode, multiply, encode, and writes `c35/trim-cand.json` (old trim times the fit, limits 0.8 to 1.2) and a predicted picture. Fit: R 0.96 over ndl 0.2 to 0.9, G 0.97 to 1.0, B 0.98 to 1.0; at the terminator knots R 0.73 and 0.79, G 0.93, B 1.10 and 1.04 (knot 16 hits the 0.8 limit). Predicted on 1755: over luma 60 +0.55 R, +0.36 G, +0.57 B (now +3.13, +1.48, +1.04); under luma 20 unchanged. **To do after the measure of the READY head**: copy the candidate to `src/stops/earth/trim.json`, build, final 1755, read the same numbers, at most one more pass; then finals 1857, 2156, draft bytes of 2010, G5 and G17; its own commit with the fit script in `assets-src/earth` and RECIPE.md; offer late, the Builder decides. The micro contrast (G17 1.198 / 1.138) is a second term: `uBloomPre` in `globe.ts` divides by `1 - bloomStrength`, which lifts detail finer than its reach as `ground.bloom` did on the terrain before 903a60ae.
- **Ending frames**: the director's shot at 5085 and 5235 holds Neptune's set only (`c35/sets.ts FRAMES`), so no change of mine can move them.

# MAIL 215: the place (branch `agent/earth-place`), from 06:41 UTC 23 November

After any compaction: read `assets-src/shared/STOP-AGENT.md` if it still binds (check), `cut/HANDOVER.md` whole, and this notebook from this heading to the end.

**MAIL 215 from the Builder (/root), verbatim**: "MAIL 215 from the Builder (/root). A new task for you, earth_builder. It replaces anything you still held. Answer with one line as soon as your new branch exists: "ACK 215 <branch> <commit>".

What happened since your last work. The film was frozen (main, commit 567b3a55), the record round ran, the master, the nine clips and the nine posts were made. Brandon watched it and wrote this note at 05:57 UTC on 2026-11-23. It outranks the kit's cover rules:

"The main issue I have is the transitions between the planet view and the moment before the surface view comes in, it's a blurry mess, and it's obviously a transition. I think the goal would be to make it seamless. Like there's actually a place on the planet that the camera's going to. Hey next, the surface for Jupiter, and Saturn both look completely weird. It looks like an image that is being stretched and contorted it shouldn't look like that. It should look like it's actually generated out of code."

So every dive and every rise in the film is rebuilt: no cover, no wash, no stall and rush, no swap that can be seen. The spec is cut/HANDOVER.md on main. Part B of it is yours for earth.

Setup
- git fetch origin main, then git checkout -b agent/earth-place FETCH_HEAD. Main holds the kept build. Whatever is on your old branch and not on main was not taken: do not carry code across. Carry your notebook across if yours is newer than main's (assets-src/earth/NOTES.md), and commit it first.
- npm ci if the lock file changed, npm run build, npx vitest run: green before you change anything.
- Read whole, from disk: cut/HANDOVER.md; kit/PROGRESS.md section "Review note of 2026-11-23" and the open items 157, 105, 118, 54 and 55 (the critics' words on your dive and rise in the frozen build); AGENTS.md and kit/addenda/ again; your own notebook.
- Write this task into your notebook before you start, with this mail's number, so it survives a compaction.

The task, in this order
1. S1 now: solve the camera of your approved close image (references/approved/earth-close.jpg) against the sphere at your spot and write src/stops/earth/place.json. Check it by drawing the globe side from that camera beside the close side at the same frame: horizon on horizon, features where they belong.
2. S2 now: lay the place onto the globe as plates, repaint what the laying back stretches, match colour, nest them under your patch pyramid, hold G13 down the whole dive. Proof: the 12 frame top down dive sheet with no blur.
3. S3: one steady move for dive and rise by the rules of E3. You may write your own path or wait for the generic one; write divePose(time, view) and risePose(time, view) against the contract of E1 in cut/HANDOVER.md either way.
4. S4 and S5 when I tell you that E1 and E2 are on main (the engine is being built now by another agent, handover_engineer, and proven on Mars first): merge main, opt in, remove your cover, bring light, exposure, haze, Sun and sky to one function of time through T, and run npm run handover for dive and rise.

What may not change: your hold segment and your terrain segment. Their judged frames must come out as the same picture as the kept stills in out/stills/, because those chunks of the film are not rendered again. The travel after your rise is not rendered again either: your rise must end in the pose, the speed and the look that travel starts with today. The timeline does not change.

Your stop
- Frames: hold 1680 to 1829, dive 1830 to 1919, terrain 1920 to 2099, rise 2100 to 2174. Judged: 1755, 1857, 2010, 2156.
- The place is the ocean with its islands, clouds and glint from low orbit, as the close image shows it. Everyone knows this planet: the islands and cloud fields of the close view must be findable on the globe from far up, and the glint must sit where the Sun puts it on both sides.
- The old cover was a veil of high cloud. It goes. Clouds may be in the scene as clouds: with structure and parallax, the place seen through or round them, never a white frame.
- What the critics wrote about the frozen build: "The globe's silhouette flattens conspicuously in just a few consecutive frames while its apex barely moves. The broad cyan halo also collapses to a thin line, so the visible horizon gives away the handoff even at peak cloud cover."; of the rise: "On the way back the horizon rapidly bows into a globe and the blue rim blooms while the surface is still whited out. **Facts checked in code (the base of the plan)**
- Close side: its local frame is the camera frame of the judged frame 2010 (camera at the origin, identity rotation at the shutter centre). The sea sphere has centre `nadir * (radius + 1)` and radius 32.5197 camera heights (`relief.json`), so 1 local unit is 1 camera height, 1 / 32.5197 of the body radius. `terrain(tRel)` is a level glide: position (side 0.3, rise 0.237, forward 0.44) times u, no height change of note, about 0.0019 heights per frame at 1920 and 0.0045 at 2100.
- `film.ts` gives the terrain view the rotation `frame.rotation * turn(time) * terrainFrame` with `terrainFrame = basis(heading x normal, normal, -heading)`, so the terrain view's local Y is the spot's normal. The close camera looks 28.35 degrees below its local horizontal, so the Sun and sky of the terrain view sit 28.35 degrees pitched against true geometry. They must stay (terrain is frozen): `spot {0.52, 0.46}` and `headingDeg 52.25` do not move.
- Spot in body coordinates: lon -77.4757, lat 18.1499 (matches `assets.ts`). Heading azimuth in the body frame: -50.59 degrees from north (toward the north west). The close frame's centre meets the sea 3.46 degrees ahead of the nadir at lon -80.33 lat 20.33 (called P below). The frame bottom is 1.45 degrees ahead, the canvas bottom 1.02, the horizon 14.03.
- Globe: `set.ts` wraps globe and air in the `shape` matrix (about 1.0043, 0.9908, 0.9975 with shear), so the drawn globe is not the unit sphere, up to 1 percent of the radius, a third of the close camera's height. Plan: bring `shape` to identity as the camera goes down (function of height), so the twin is true at the place.
- Patches: gnomonic about one centre (the spot), 96, 48, 24, 12 degrees at 4096 px; off at the hold. No live glint in the globe shader. Baked patches switch off the live cloud.
- `Plan.clock` rewinds the spin by 11.5 s at the rise cover centre, and `set.ts` reads `clock(time) !== time` as "after the rise". Travel needs the rewound state at 2175, so the rewind must become a smooth function of time across the rise (the critics saw the snap: item 118).

Laid back north up about the nadir (degrees east, north of the nadir, view azimuth -50.59): the long island runs from its west tip at (-1.68, 1.78) to its east end at (0.05, 2.96), 2.09 degrees long, axis azimuth 56; a round bank at (-2.0, 2.5); a bank with a long cay from (-1.74, 2.82) to (-0.91, 3.10); three islets at (-0.25 to 0.11, 3.25 to 3.57); the glint lane from 2 to 5.4 degrees out, 3 to 4 degrees left of the view axis; open sea with towers to about 6 degrees; cloud bands and haze from 6 degrees to the horizon at 14. The pyramid (generated, close to the real map) has, in degrees east and north of the spot: the small island south of the large one with centre (-2.44, 1.13), 1.17 long, axis azimuth 101; the large island as a band from (0.19, 2.34) to (-7.5, 5.3); the peninsula of the island east of it from (-0.09, 0.52). At the spot the twin looks straight at both.

**Decision (in `place.json`, block `globe`).** The twin's nadir is at lon -79.3 lat 16.492 (1.75 degrees west and 1.65 south of the spot), view azimuth -50.594 as before. The plan's `spot` and `headingDeg` stay, because the terrain view's Sun and sky hang on them. Then the long island of the image lies on the small island of the pyramid, the large island fills only the far field of the frame beyond 5.5 degrees, where the image has cloud bands, and its nearest coast begins just outside the right edge of the close canvas. The Sun is 4.4 degrees left of the view (azimuth -55.0 at frame 1911, elevation 12.4 degrees), where the image has its glare (3 degrees left). Overlays: `assets-src/earth/place/overlay.py EAST NORTH AZ NAME` (the pyramid's land drawn into the close canvas), three placements tried (`ov-c`, `ov-m`, `ov-e`), the middle one taken.

**What the pyramid must change for it, all in the first level (96 degrees), so every deeper level agrees exactly and the change is a few px on screen while the level fades in**: the small island turned from azimuth 101 to 56, lengthened from 1.17 to 2.09 degrees and moved 0.43 degrees; convective cloud lines over the large island where the image has cloud bands; the banks and islets north of the long island.

**Rejected.** The spot itself (two islands in the near field). A view azimuth that fits the small island as it lies (-5 degrees): the Sun would stand 50 degrees left of the view and the glint would leave the frame. Other islands near the spot: axis or neighbours worse. A new island that the hold does not show: not findable from far up.

The difference lowpass(G) minus lowpass(H) is the tone texture `place-tone.bin` (512 x 512, float16, code value difference / 255, alpha is the edge mask) and is added in the display code domain by nearness (`uNear`, the descent ramp of `look.ts`). ## 08:17 to 08:35 UTC: E1 merged, S3, the path

- Merged origin/main add6ccbe (E1 at 33ccccb6) as 9a27f0c0. Read `cut/HANDOVER.md`, `assets-src/shared/HANDOVER-NOTES.md`, `src/director/handover.ts`, `moves.ts`, `film.ts`.
- **Opt in**: `params.handover = place.json path.handover`. Dive: `at 0.99`, 1 dissolve frame, 12 place frames, so T is at 1919.25 and the place window is 1907 to 1919. Rise: `at 0.005` (the engine wants `at` above 0), T at 2100.25, place window 2100 to 2112. So all 90 frames of the dive and all 75 of the rise are the globe side; the frame of T is half and half through the shutter, at the twin.
- **Close side on dive and rise**: the level glide of the terrain view carried on in time, `terrain(time - terrain.start)`. The old entry and exit terms and the cover values are gone from `params`.
- **Globe side** (`plan.ts`, values in `place.json` block `path`): anchor = `twin(glide(t), t)`. Height = anchor height times exp(L(x)), x frames from T. L comes from `law(frames, ease, total, last)`: the rate is 0 at T, eases over `ease[0]` frames to a constant, and over `ease[1]` frames into `last`, the hold's own rate for the dive and the travel's for the rise; `total` is ln(far height / anchor height). With k = L / total: nadir = slerp(anchor nadir, far direction) over `over`, aim = lerp(P, far aim) over `aim`, up = slerp(anchor up, far up) over `turn`, vfov by k, each with a quintic ease; orientation by `lookAt`. P is where the anchor's optical axis meets the unit sphere.
- **Clock**: the spin rewind of 11.5 s (12.65 degrees), a jump at the cover centre before, is now `time - 11.5 * smooth(ramp(T + rewind / fps, rise.end, time))`: equal to `time` through frame 2100 and to `time - 11.5` from 2175, so hold, terrain and travel see the states they saw.
- **Veil**: `veil.ts`, `look.veil` and all its wiring in `set.ts` removed. "After the rise" in `set.ts` is now `time > handoverOf('earth', 'rise').time` instead of a comparison of `clock(time)` with `time`.
- **Tools removed**: `assets-src/earth/dive/show.ts`, `join.ts`, `canvas.ts` used the old plan exports `timing` and `Tune` (see the list in the commit).
- **Tools new**: `assets-src/earth/place/path.ts` prints the path per frame (height, rate, where P is on screen, off nadir angle, limb angle, vfov, turn per frame) and the joints, and writes `out/work/earth/place/path.json`. `down.ts` renders the top down dive sheet frames (`?earthSide=down`: camera straight above P at the real path's height, real up and vfov), `downsheet.py NAME COLS` lays the sheet.
- **Measured by `path.ts` (08:33)**. Dive: ln of the height ratio 4.6909 over 89.25 frames, mean 0.05155 per frame, largest 0.06868, largest over mean 1.332, rate at one fifth of the way (frame 1848) 0.06782, largest second difference of the rate 9.8e-4, largest turn 2.0 degrees per frame. Rise: 4.7568, mean 0.06259, largest 0.09252, largest over mean 1.478, rate one fifth before the end (frame 2160) 0.06256, largest second difference 1.09e-3, largest turn 2.8 degrees per frame. Joints: hold into dive, dive into twin at T, twin into rise at T, rise into travel: position apart 0 to 1e-17, orientation apart 0, speed apart 1.5e-9, 2.0e-9, 3.5e-7, 9.3e-8 per frame. P on screen: within 0.49 to 0.51 across and 0.44 to 0.50 down in the dive, 0.42 to 0.50 down in the rise.

## 08:46 to 09:15 UTC: S2, second pass on the plates and the limb

What the first sheets showed and what was changed for each.

- Pink flat blobs and embossed dark sea at the frame bottom, frames 1883 to 1905. Two causes. One: the stored levels held `G - lowpass(G) + lowpass(H)` and clipped at 255 per channel, so white cloud lost green when the tone was added back. Two: the south east of the plate (behind the camera of the close view) was still the old unfinished patch content.
  - Level scheme, second version. The plate `place-12.jpg` holds G as it is. The levels 24, 48, 96 hold `hold * (1 - w) + G * w`, w the footprint of the plate with its feather. `place-tone.bin` holds only the wide grade of the sea, `lowpass(graded(H) - H)`. The shader adds `(near - inside * laid) * tone` in display code values, `laid` the share of the pixel that comes from any level, so the footprint shows G minus the grade while the camera is high and G itself when it is low. No stored file clips now.
  - Take 3 of the plate (prompt `assets-src/earth/place/prompt-p12-south.txt`, inputs `out/work/earth/place/q12-in-1024.jpg` and `close-1408.jpg`): cumulus and smooth sea in the south east. It also removed land the hold map has, so only a polygon is taken from it: `assets-src/earth/place/mend.py south`, polygon in `mend.json`.
- The far zone. At the twin the close view shows a bright cloud sheet from about 120 px below the horizon up to it (6 to 12 degrees of ground ahead of the nadir), no land. The plate had sparse cloud, the tip of the land in the south west and the west of the long island in the north there, because takes 1 and 2 turned the laid back white smear into sea and kept the land of the hold map on top of it.
  - Takes 4 and 5 (prompt `prompt-p12-far.txt`, input `far-in-1024.jpg`: the plate with the laid back picture put back beyond 5 degrees from the nadir): a cloud sheet seen from above. Take 4 kept (brighter, more structure; take 5 is bluer and softer). Only the far zone is taken, with a mask that follows the cloud brightness of the take so the sheet ends in clumps and not on the straight edges of the canvas: `mend.py far`.
  - The land under the sheet is still on the hold map, so from far up the long island is whole and during the dive its west goes under cloud. To keep that from happening large in frame the place comes in early: `descent.place [3.2, 1.4]`, a ramp in ln height that forces the first level in inside the footprint (`uPlaceIn`). At the hold height 3.366 it is 0, so the hold is untouched; at height 1.4 the 12 degree footprint is about 200 px wide.
- The limb. Numbers at frame 1915 (corrected 09:26 with the texel view, the first note here had a wrong row for the sphere): the limb of the sphere as the twin sees it is at row 199 in the centre column (203 at x 1440, 205 at 1480, 219 at 1680, 238 at 480, 272 at 240). The close view's change from blue to pale sits on that row. The hard edge that `check.py` finds on the close side, row 203.6 to 205, is the top of its cloud sheet, 4.6 px below the limb. The globe side's bright edge was at 196.6 to 198.2: that is not the sphere, it is the air's `line` term, a Gaussian 0.0016 radii wide centred 0.0009 below the limb, which is a hairline from the hold and a 17 px white band at the twin (1 px there is 1.82e-4 radii of tangent height). So the 4 to 6 px I reported as a horizon error after M1 is the line, not the pose; no trim of the twin is made. New `descent` values scale the line (`line`), the inner rim (`inner`) and the glow heights (`air`) apart, and `fog` is a floor for the sky near the ground (the close view's sky is dark blue, 3..9, 8..22, 16..33, not black). Stars near the ground are off (`light.skyLow` value 0).
- For tuning there is a development hook, `tuned()` in `src/stops/earth/place.ts`: the query `earthTune=path:value;...` overrides numbers of `look.descent`. `sides.ts --tune ... --tag NAME` uses it, `rim.py` prints the rim rows of each variant beside the close side. The hook goes before the handover is reported.

Now, inside the plate, the tone is the real difference plate minus hold in the low frequencies over open sea (`mend.json` block `tone`): from far up the plate carries the hold's sea, and the low view's dark sea comes in with nearness, as a look that changes with height. The plate's far zone is the repaint (a sheet seen from above). From the close camera's direction the true picture of that ground is the lay back itself. ## MAIL 258 from the Builder (low priority; verbatim)

MAIL 258 from the Builder (/root) to every stop builder.
Main moved to da69509a. New in the globe shader and the loader, all off unless your stop asks (notes: assets-src/shared/GLOBE-NOTES.md):
- `ctx.texture(path, {srgb: false, channels: 1})`: a one channel texture, a quarter of the memory. Use it for every mask and height map you load, if your set is near its memory limit.
- `PatchSet.own?: boolean[]`: a patch level with its own light (a plate that already carries the light of the close view is not lit a second time).
- `PatchSet.fadeMinor?: boolean`: a level fades in by its fine axis on screen, so a level seen at a grazing angle does not come in as a hard strip.
MAX_PATCHES is 10.
No frame of main changed (115 draft frames with the same bytes). Merge origin/main into your branch at your next commit. Nothing else changes for you: go on with the list of my last mail to you.

## MAIL 275 from the Builder (about 13:14 UTC, high priority, no answer needed; verbatim)

From the Builder (/root) to every stop builder and coder. Mail 275: how your stop gets to its critic rounds. No answer needed unless a point below is red for you.

1. A stop is taken onto main and its round 1 starts (rounds are numbered on from 37, up to three per stop, every critic each round) when four things hold on one commit that you name:
 a. both handovers run with no cover and read as one place (Jupiter and Saturn: also the surface view from code; the Sun: the surface view with its new motion and the rise);
 b. the machine checks are green on every frame you changed: G5, G13 and G17 as the harness gives them, and the handover report of `npm run handover -- --stop <id>`;
 c. the frames you may not change keep their bytes (your hold and your surface view where it is approved, at the draft tier everywhere and at the final tier on the judged frames);
 d. build, tsc and tests are green on a merge of the newest main.
 It does not have to be finished. What is still wrong goes to the critics with the rest. Do not wait for a perfect state: when a to d hold, send me "READY <stop> <commit>" with the list of what you still see as wrong.
2. Takes of generated assets: kit/06-ASSETS.md gives six takes per asset per round, and six per phase for an asset made outside a round. A mail of mine with findings is not a round. If an asset has used its six in this phase, keep its best take and say so in your READY line. In a round each asset has six again where a critic asks for a change. Work by code has no take limit.
3. I render every commit you name at every frame on the render machines and write what I see. Push your code branch whenever your looks show something you want judged, and name the commit.

## 12:41 to 13:20 UTC: the cloud field as bodies, the calm, the wipes, the hold under the field (MAIL 254, 2 to 4; MAIL 259, 5 and 6)

- `deck.py` cuts the four takes into bodies (matte by the darkest channel against the local top, cast shadow map from the lit sea round each body, turned so the shadows fall the way the Sun of the place throws them) and lays them out on a grid with a seeded shuffle over 24 degrees about P (8192 px), each body kept or dropped by the density under it. Output `field-alpha.npy`, `field-colour.npy`, `field-shade.npy`, `field-density.png` in `out/work/earth/place/`. `deck-3.log`: cover of the takes 0.194, 0.171, 0.203, 0.195; 57 of 90 spots placed; cover where the density is over 0.9: 0.434. The first layout was too dense and grey (cover 0.761); `step` 0.55, `density.full` 0.8, `shadow.onCloud` 0.25 since.
- Density: the cloud of the ring take outside the plate, the plate's own far cloud inside it (from 5.0 to 6.4 degrees from the nadir).
- `levels.py` rewritten. The field is laid over every level, the plate and the fine level, so a cloud mass ends in bodies and the bodies throw shadows on the sea. The plate gives way to the hold's own levels (12 where it reaches, 24 beyond, through `seaGain`) by the density and by the far ramp; the far zone of the plate was a repaint with dots and streak blobs. The old composite of the ring sheet is gone.
- `calm.py`: the dot pattern on land (reads as lights by day). Opening by a disc, then a median, on land only (green or pale ground near, warmer than the sea, away from cloud wider than the disc). A parameter named `r` clashed with the red channel in the first version; renamed `dot`.
- Wipes (`mend.json` `wipe`): marks of the repaint in the plate, painted over from their surround.
- `globe.ts`: uniform `uLowSize`, so the texel measure counts the low plate's own density where the low look shows. The `tuned()` hook is out of `place.ts` and `set.ts`.
- First test frames (draft, `out/work/earth/place/m3/`): the field over the Gulf and Yucatan reads as bodies with shadows and ends in bodies; the far bank at the top of 1890 and 1900 has cumulus detail. Wrong: field bodies outside the low plate read pink to mauve in the top corners of 1890, 1900, 2124, 2129; two soft white wedges at 1890 and 1900; the cream patch of the glint.
- The mauve, cause: the tone texture (what the plate's sea differs from the hold's by) was added on the grey flanks of clouds; its cloud gate looked at the darkest channel only. Fix: the tone also needs colour, `plates.tone.grey` [0.2, 0.45] (spread of the three channels over the brightest), uniform `uToneGrey`. After it the corners of 1890, 1900, 2124 and 2129 are white and grey.
- The wedges, cause: two marks of the repaint in the plate at plate px (3020, 2168) and (3121, 2031); the disc of the wipe was smaller than the mark, so the fill started from the mark's own rim. The wipe now takes everything brighter than `bright` inside `reach` times the radius, grown by `growPx`.
